f_{\hbar}=\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}+\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}b^{4}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}},

\upsilon_{\hbar}=\frac{\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}},

S_{i}=\Gamma^{*}_{i,d}
and
c_{i}=\xi_{i}^{*}
for
i=1
and
2,
all the conditions in Proposition
2.22
are satisfied respectively.

Indeed, by Proposition
3.1
(3),
\xi^{*}_{i}
is a critical point of
f=\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}
in
B_{i,d},
hence condition (i) is satisfied.

By (
3.44
),
\xi_{i}^{*}
is the unique maximum point of
\mathrm{Re}f=\frac{\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}}{2\pi}
on
\Gamma^{*}_{i},
hence condition (ii) is satisfied.

For conditions (iii) and (iv), since
\xi^{*}_{i}\in D_{\epsilon,c},

\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{i}^{*})
is a finite value. As a consequence,
g(\xi_{i}^{*})=\exp\big(\frac{\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{i}^{*})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)\neq 0,
and condition (iv) is satisfied; and by Proposition
3.1
(3), condition (iii) is satisfied.

For condition (v), by Proposition
3.11
,
|\upsilon_{\hbar}(\xi)|=\big|\frac{\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\big|<\frac{N}{2\pi}
on
B_{i,d}.

For condition (vi), since near each
\xi_{i},

\Gamma^{*}
is obtained from a straight line segment
\Gamma^{\prime}
by moving along the flow lines of a smooth vector field
\mathbf{v},

\Gamma^{*}_{i,d}
is a smooth embedding around
\xi^{*}_{i}.

Finally, by Proposition
2.22
, Proposition
3.1
(3) and Proposition
3.2
, we have as
b\to 0,

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{1,d}}\exp\bigg(\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\xi=&\frac{e^{\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}}}{\sqrt{\sqrt{-\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l})}}}\Big(1+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Big)\\
=&\frac{e^{-\big(\frac{\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})}{2\pi b^{2}}+\frac{\pi}{4}\big)\mathbf{i}}}{\sqrt[4]{\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l})}}e^{\frac{-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l})}{\pi b^{2}}}\Big(1+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Big),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{2,d}}\exp\bigg(\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\xi=&\frac{e^{\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{2}^{*})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}}}{\sqrt{-\sqrt{-\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l})}}}\Big(1+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Big)\\
=&\frac{e^{\big(\frac{\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})}{2\pi b^{2}}+\frac{\pi}{4}\big)\mathbf{i}}}{\sqrt[4]{\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l})}}e^{\frac{-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l})}{\pi b^{2}}}\Big(1+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Big).\end{split}

This completes the proof of (I).

The proof of (II) and (III) is quite similar to that of Case (a). Namely, we have

\begin{split}&\bigg|\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}}\exp\bigg(\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\xi\bigg|\\
\leqslant&\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\xi|.\end{split}

For (II), by (
3.44
) and Proposition
3.1
(3),
\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)<-2\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})
for all
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\{\xi^{*}_{1},\xi^{*}_{2}\};
and by the compactness of
{\Gamma^{*}_{L}}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{d},
there exists an
\epsilon>0
such that

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)<-2\mathrm{Col}(\bm{l})-4\epsilon

(3.45)

for all
\xi\in{\Gamma^{*}_{L}}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{d}.
Also by the compactness, there is an
M>0
such that

\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)<M

(3.46)

for all
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}_{L}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{d}.
By Proposition
3.11
, there is a
b_{1}>0
such that

\mathrm{Im}\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}<Nb^{4}<\epsilon

(3.47)

for all
b<b_{1}
and
\xi\in\Gamma^{*};
and together with (
3.45
), we have

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}<-2\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-4\epsilon+\epsilon<-2\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-2\epsilon

(3.48)

for all
b<b_{1}
and
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}_{L}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{d}.
Putting (
3.46
) and (
3.48
) together, we have

\begin{split}&\bigg|\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{L}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{d}}\exp\bigg(\frac{U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\xi\bigg|\\
<&\frac{2(L-d)e^{\frac{M}{2\pi}}}{\pi b}\exp\bigg(\frac{-\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-\epsilon}{\pi b^{2}}\bigg)\\
<&O\Big(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-\epsilon_{3}}{\pi b^{2}}}\Big)\end{split}

for any
\epsilon_{3}<\epsilon.
This completes the proof of (II).

For (III), there is a
b_{0}\in(0,b_{1})
such that for all
b<b_{0},

\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}L)b^{2}<\epsilon,

(3.49)

Kb^{2}<\epsilon
and
Nb^{2}<\epsilon,
where
K
and
N
are respectively the constants in Propositions
3.10
and
3.11
. We claim that, for
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}
and
b<b_{0},

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}<-2\big(\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})+\epsilon\big)-(4\pi-\epsilon)\big(|\xi-\xi_{\text{mid}}|-L\big),

(3.50)

as a consequence of which, we have,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\pi b}\int_{\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}(\xi)b^{4}}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\xi|\\
<&\frac{1}{\pi b}\exp\bigg(\frac{-\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-\epsilon}{\pi b^{2}}\bigg)\int_{\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}}\exp\bigg(\frac{-(4\pi-\epsilon)\big(|\xi-\xi_{\text{mid}}|-L\big)}{2\pi}\bigg)|d\xi|\\
<&O\Big(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-\epsilon_{3}}{\pi b^{2}}}\Big)\end{split}

(3.51)

for any
\epsilon_{3}<\epsilon.
For the proof of the claim, by Proposition
3.6
and (
3.45
), for
l>L,
i.e.,
\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}l\in\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L},
we have

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}l)\leqslant\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}L)<-2\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-4\epsilon;

(3.52)

and by Proposition
3.6
again and the choice of
L
and
b_{0},
we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\Big(\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi^{*}+\mathbf{i}l)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi^{*}+\mathbf{i}l)b^{2}\Big)<-4\pi+\epsilon,

and

\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\Big(\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi^{*}-\mathbf{i}l)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi^{*}-\mathbf{i}l)b^{2}\Big)>4\pi-\epsilon.

Together with the Mean Value Theorem, (
3.52
) and (
3.49
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi)b^{2}<&\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi^{*}\pm\mathbf{i}L)+\mathrm{Im}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}L)b^{2}-(4\pi-\epsilon)\big|\xi-(\xi_{\text{mid}}\pm\mathbf{i}L)\big|\\
<&-2\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l})-3\epsilon-(4\pi-\epsilon)\big(|\xi-\xi_{\text{mid}}|-L\big)\end{split}

(3.53)

for all
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}.
Finally, putting (
3.53
) and (
3.47
) together, we have (
3.50
) and the first inequality in (
3.51
); and since

|\xi-\xi_{\text{mid}}|-L\to+\infty

as
\xi\in\Gamma^{*}{\smallsetminus}\Gamma^{*}_{L}
approaches
\infty,
we have the second inequality in (
3.51
). This completes the proof of (III).

Putting (I), (II) and (III) together, we have

\bigg\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}\\
a_{4}&a_{5}&a_{6}\end{matrix}\bigg\}_{b}=\frac{e^{\frac{-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l})}{\pi b^{2}}}}{\sqrt[4]{\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l})}}\Bigg(2\cos\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})}{2\pi b^{2}}+\frac{\pi}{4}\bigg)+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Bigg).

(3.54)

If
\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})=0
, then we have

\limsup_{b\to 0}\pi b^{2}\log\Bigg|\bigg\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}\\
a_{4}&a_{5}&a_{6}\end{matrix}\bigg\}_{b}\Bigg|=\lim_{b\to 0}\pi b^{2}\log\Bigg|\bigg\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}\\
a_{4}&a_{5}&a_{6}\end{matrix}\bigg\}_{b}\Bigg|=-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l});

and if
\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})\neq 0
, then we take a sequence
b_{n}\to 0
such that
\frac{\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})}{2\pi b_{n}^{2}}+\frac{\pi}{4}=0
(mod
\pi
) for each
n
, and have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\pi b_{n}^{2}\log\bigg\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}\\
a_{4}&a_{5}&a_{6}\end{matrix}\bigg\}_{b_{n}}=-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l}),

and

\limsup_{b\to 0}\pi b^{2}\log\Bigg|\bigg\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}\\
a_{4}&a_{5}&a_{6}\end{matrix}\bigg\}_{b}\Bigg|=-\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l})

in Case (b).
∎

Remark 3.12
.

In
[
36
]
, we will prove Eq. (
1.4
) by showing that
\mathrm{Re}U_{\bm{\alpha}}(\xi_{1}^{*})
in (
3.54
) equals
-2\mathrm{Cov(\Delta)}
, where
\Delta
is the truncated hyperideal tetrhadron in the anti-de Sitter space
\mathbb{A}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{S}^{3}
with
(l_{1},\dots,l_{6})
the edge lengths, and
\mathrm{Cov(\Delta)}
is the co-volume of
\Delta
as defined in Remark
1.4
.

4
State-integral convergence and asymptotics

Let
M
be a hyperbolic
3
-manifold with a totally geodesic boundary with an ideal triangulation
\mathcal{T}.
Let
E
and
T
respectively be the sets of edges and tetrahedra of
\mathcal{T}.
For
e\in E,
let
\alpha_{e}=\pi ba_{e}-\frac{\pi b^{2}}{2}.
For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let
\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}=(\alpha_{e_{1}},\dots,\alpha_{e_{6}})
and let
\xi_{\Delta}=\pi bu_{\Delta}.
Let
U_{b}\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)=U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},b}(\xi_{\Delta})

be as defined in (
3.6
) and let
U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)=U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})

be as defined in (
3.3
).

Then by (
3.6
), writing
\sinh(\frac{l_{e}}{2\pi b^{2}})
as
\frac{1}{2}\big(\exp(\frac{l_{e}}{2\pi b^{2}})-\exp(-\frac{l_{e}}{2\pi b^{2}})\big)
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})=\frac{(-2\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\sum_{\bm{\lambda}\in\{-1,1\}^{E}}\int_{\Gamma}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\lambda_{e}\sinh l_{e}\bigg)\exp\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{W}_{b}^{\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi},\end{split}

(4.1)

where the contour

\Gamma=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times\mathbb{C}^{T}\ \bigg|\ \xi_{\Delta}\in\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\text{ for all }\Delta\text{ in }T\bigg\}

and
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
is the contour of integral in (
3.6
) that
will be specified later,
d\bm{\alpha}=\prod_{e\in E}d\alpha_{e},

d\bm{\xi}=\prod_{\Delta\in T}d\xi_{\Delta},
and

\begin{split}\mathcal{W}_{b}^{\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=&2\pi\sum_{e\in E}\lambda_{e}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)\\
=&\mathcal{W}^{\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}+\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}\end{split}

(4.2)

with

\mathcal{W}^{\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=2\pi\sum_{e\in E}\lambda_{e}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big),

\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{\Delta\in T}\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})

with
\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}}
computed in (
3.4
), and

\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{\Delta\in T}\nu_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},b}(\xi_{\Delta})

with
\nu_{\bm{\alpha},b}
as in (
3.6
).
To simplify the notations, we write
\mathcal{W}_{b}\doteq\mathcal{W}_{b}^{\bm{\lambda}}
and
\mathcal{W}\doteq\mathcal{W}^{\bm{\lambda}}
when
\bm{\lambda}=(1,\dots,1).

By (
3.6
) again, we can also write

\begin{split}\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})=\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\sum_{\bm{\mu}\in\{-1,1\}^{E}}\sum_{\bm{\lambda}\in\{-1,1\}^{E}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\mu_{e}\lambda_{e}\bigg)\int_{\Gamma}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi},\end{split}

(4.3)

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=&2\pi\sum_{e\in E}(\mu_{e}b^{2}+\lambda_{e})(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)\\
=&\mathcal{V}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}+\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}\end{split}

(4.4)

with

\mathcal{V}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=2\pi\sum_{e\in E}(\mu_{e}b^{2}+\lambda_{e})(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big).

To simplify the notations, we write
\mathcal{V}_{b}\doteq\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}
and
\mathcal{V}\doteq\mathcal{V}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}
when
\bm{\mu}=\bm{\lambda}=(1,\dots,1).

We prove Theorem
1.6
on the convergence of the integral (
1.7
) in Section
4.2
, and Theorem
1.9
on the asymptotics of
\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M)
in Section
4.3
. All the preliminary results are established in Section
4.1
.
To prove Theorem
1.6
, we bound
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})
by an auxiliary function, whose existence is ensured by Luo-Yang’s result
[
38
]
on the relationship between angle structures and hyperbolic polyhedral metrics. In fact, in Section
4.2
, we prove a stronger result that the convergence holds for all the ideal triangulations that admit angle structures (see Theorem
4.9
).
To establish the asymptotics in Theorem
1.9
, we apply the saddle point approximations to the integral in (
4.3
); and the style of the argument is similar to those in Section
3
. A major difference is that the integral under consideration here is with multi-variables
\xi_{\Delta}
’s and the
l_{e}
’s. To establish the desired properties of the relevant functions, we further employ geometric tools including the Murakami-Yano volume formula in the form of
[
6
]
and the Wong-Yang formula
[
75
]
of the adjoint twisted Reidemeister torsions.

4.1
Properties of relevant functions

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a Kojima ideal triangulation of
M
with edge lengths
\bm{l}^{*}=(l_{e}^{*}),
which is a generalized hyperbolic polyhedral metric with all the cone angles
2\pi.
(See subsection
2.6
.)

For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let

\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{e_{1}}}{2},\dots,\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{e_{6}}}{2}\Big).

Guaranteed by Proposition
3.1
,

(1)

if
(l^{*}_{e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{e_{6}})\in\mathcal{L},
then we let
\xi^{*}_{\Delta}
be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}
in
D;
and

(2)

if
(l^{*}_{e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{e_{6}})\in\partial\mathcal{L},
then we let
\xi^{*}_{\Delta}
be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}
on
\partial D.

By Proposition
3.1
(2),
\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}=2\pi
in case (2).

Let
\overline{D}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ \frac{3\pi}{2}\leqslant\mathrm{Re}\xi\leqslant 2\pi\}.
Then we have the following

Proposition 4.1
.

The function

\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=2\pi\sum_{e\in E}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)

has a critical point

\bm{z}^{*}=\bigg(\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E},\big(\xi^{*}_{\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}\bigg)

in
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}\times\overline{D}^{T}

with the critical value

\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})=-2\mathbf{i}\mathrm{Vol}(M).

Proof.

Let
\bm{z}^{*}=(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*}),
where
\bm{\alpha}^{*}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E}
and
\bm{\xi}^{*}=\big(\xi^{*}_{\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}.
For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let
l^{*}_{\Delta}=(l^{*}_{e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{e_{6}}).

We first have

\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})}=\frac{\partial U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{\xi^{*}_{\Delta}}=0.

(4.5)

Nest, let
W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})=U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi^{*}_{\Delta})
be the function defined in (
3.11
). Then for any edge
e
of
\Delta,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}=&\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})}+\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})}\cdot\frac{\partial\xi^{*}_{\Delta}}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}\\
=&\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})}.\end{split}

As a consequence, by (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) we have

\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})}=\frac{\partial W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta}}=-4\frac{\partial\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l}_{\Delta})}{\partial l_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{\Delta}}=-2\widetilde{\theta}_{(\Delta,e)},

where

\widetilde{\theta}_{(\Delta,e)}
is the extended dihedral angle of the
\bm{l}^{*}_{\Delta}
at the edge
e.
Then for each
e\in E,

\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})}=4\pi+\sum_{\Delta\in T}\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})}=2\Big(2\pi-\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\widetilde{\theta}_{(\Delta,e)}\Big)=0,

(4.6)

where the last summation is over all the corners around
e,
and the last equality comes from the fact that the sum of the dihedral angles around an edge equals is the cone angle at this edge, which equals
2\pi.
By (
4.5
) and (
4.6
),
(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})
is a critical point of
\mathcal{W}.

Finally, for the critical value, by Proposition
3.1
, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})=&2\pi\mathbf{i}\sum_{e\in E}l^{*}_{e}-2\mathbf{i}\sum_{\Delta\in T}\Big(\mathrm{Vol}(\Delta)+\sum_{e\sim\Delta}\frac{\widetilde{\theta}_{(\Delta,e)}l^{*}_{e}}{2}\Big)\\
=&-2\mathbf{i}\sum_{\Delta\in T}\mathrm{Vol}(\Delta)+\mathbf{i}\sum_{e\in E}\Big(2\pi-\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\widetilde{\theta}_{(\Delta,e)}\Big)l^{*}_{e}\\
=&-2\mathbf{i}\mathrm{Vol}(M),\end{split}

where the last summation in the first row is over all the edges
e
in
\Delta,
and the last summation in the second row is over all the corners around
e.

∎

By Proposition
2.14
, there is a one-parameter family of hyperbolic polyhedral metrics
\bm{l}^{*}_{s}
converging to
\bm{l}^{*}.
For
e\in E,
let
\theta_{s,e}
be the cone angle of
\bm{l}^{*}_{s}
at
e.
For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let

\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{s,e_{1}}}{2},\dots,\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{s,e_{6}}}{2}\Big).

Guaranteed by Proposition
3.1
(1), we let
\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}=\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta})
be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}
in
D.

Proposition 4.2
.

The function

\mathcal{W}_{s}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{e\in E}\theta_{s,e}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)

has a critical point

\bm{z}_{s}^{*}=\bigg(\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{s,e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E},\big(\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}\bigg)

in
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}\times D^{T}.

Proof.

The proof follows verbatim the proof of Proposition
4.1
. Namely, let
\bm{z}_{s}^{*}=(\bm{\alpha}_{s}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{*}),
where
\bm{\alpha}_{s}^{*}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{s,e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E}
and
\bm{\xi}_{s}^{*}=\big(\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}.
For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let
l^{*}_{s,\Delta}=(l^{*}_{s,e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{s,e_{6}}).

We first have

\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{s}}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{s}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{*})}=\frac{\partial U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}}=0.

(4.7)

Next, let
W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})=U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi^{*}_{\Delta})
be the function defined in (
3.11
). Then for any edge
e
of
\Delta,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}=&\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}+\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\cdot\frac{\partial\xi^{*}_{\Delta}}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}\\
=&\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}.\end{split}

As a consequence, and by Proposition
3.1
(1),

\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}=\frac{\partial W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}=-4\frac{\partial\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l}_{\Delta})}{\partial l_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}=-2\theta_{s,(\Delta,e)},

(4.8)

where

\theta_{s,(\Delta,e)}
is the dihedral angle of the
\bm{l}^{*}_{s,\Delta}
at the edge
e.
Then for each
e\in E,

\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{s}}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{s}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{*})}=2\theta_{s,e}+\sum_{\Delta\in T}\frac{\partial U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}=2\Big(\theta_{s,e}-\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\theta_{s,(\Delta,e)}\Big)=0,

(4.9)

where the last summation is over all the corners around
e,
and the last equality comes from the fact that the sum of the dihedral angles around an edge equals is the cone angle at this edge, which equals
\theta_{s,e}.
By (
4.7
) and (
4.9
),
(\bm{\alpha}_{s}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{s}^{*})
is a critical point of
\mathcal{W}_{s}.

∎

Proposition 4.3
.

\frac{\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})\big)}{\exp\big(\frac{\kappa(\bm{z}^{*})}{\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)\prod_{e\in E}\sinh^{2}l_{e}}=\pm 2^{5|E|+|T|}\mathrm{Tor}(DM,\mathrm{Ad}_{\rho_{DM}}),

(4.10)

where
\mathrm{Tor}(DM,\mathrm{Ad}_{\rho_{DM}})
is the Reidemeister torsion of the double
DM
of
M
twisted by the adjoint action of the double
\rho_{DM}
of the holonomy representation of the hyperbolic structure on
M.
In particular,

\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})\big)\neq 0.

Proof.

Guaranteed by Proposition
2.14
, let
\{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}\}_{s\in(0,s_{0})}
be a one-parameter family of hyperbolic polyhedral metrics on
M
that converges to
\bm{l}^{*}.
Let
N
be the
3
-manifold obtained from the double
DM
of
M
by removing the double of the edges of
\mathcal{T},
and for
s\in(0,s_{0})
let
\rho_{s}
be the holonomy representation of the hyperbolic cone metric on
N
obtained by doubling
\bm{l}^{*}_{s}.
Let
\bm{m}
be the system of the meridians of a tubular neighborhood of the double of the edges, and let
\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}
be the twisted adjoint Reidemeister torsion function of the
\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb{C})
-characters of
\pi_{1}(N).

Let
\bm{z}_{s}^{*}=\Big(\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{s,e}}{2}\big)_{e\in E},\big(\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}\Big)
be the critical point of the function
\mathcal{W}_{s}
in Proposition
4.2
. We will prove that

\frac{\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*}_{s})\big)}{\exp\big(\frac{\kappa(\bm{z}^{*}_{s})}{\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)}=\pm 2^{3|E|+|T|}\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}([\rho_{s}])

(4.11)

for all
s\in(0,s_{0}).

Assuming this, then as
\det(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W})
is continuous in
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi}),

\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}
by Theorem
2.20
(i) and Remark
2.19
is analytic and hence continuous in a neighborhood of the character of the holonomy representation
\rho
of the hyperbolic cone metric on
N
(with all the cone angles
2\pi
) obtained by doubling the generalizaed hyperbolic polyhedral metric
\bm{l}^{*}
of
M,
and
\bm{z}^{*}_{s}
approaches
\bm{z}^{*}
as
s
tends to
0,
we have

\frac{\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})\big)}{\exp\big(\frac{\kappa(\bm{z}^{*})}{\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)}=\pm 2^{3|E|+|T|}\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}([\rho]).

Then by Theorem
2.20
(iii), as
DM
can be considered as obtained from
N
by doing a hyperbolic Dehn filling along the meridians
\bm{m},
and the double of the edges form the system of curves
\bm{\gamma}
with the logarithmic holonomy
\mathrm{H}(\gamma_{e})=2l_{e}
for each
e\in E,
we have

\begin{split}\frac{\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})\big)}{\exp\big(\frac{\kappa(\bm{z}^{*})}{\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)\prod_{e\in E}\sinh^{2}l_{e}}=&\pm 2^{3|E|+|T|}\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}([\rho])\prod_{e\in E}\frac{1}{\sinh^{2}l_{e}}\\
=&\pm 2^{5|E|+|T|}\mathrm{Tor}(DM,\mathrm{Ad}_{\rho_{DM}}).\end{split}

Since
\pm\mathrm{Tor}(DM,\mathrm{Ad}_{\rho_{DM}})\neq 0,
we have
\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*})\big)\neq 0,
which completes the proof.

We are left to prove (
4.11
). We use the argument adapted from that of
[
73
, Lemma 4.3]
. Namely, for
\Delta\in T
and
e\in E
we denote by
\Delta\sim e
and
e\sim\Delta
if the tetrahedron
\Delta
intersects the edge
e;
and for
\{e_{i},e_{j}\}\subset E
we denote by
\Delta\sim e_{i},e_{j}
if
\Delta
intersects both
e_{i}
and
e_{j}.
Let
(\theta_{e})_{e\in E}
be the cone angle functions in terms of the edge lengths of
M.
Then we claim:

(1)

For
\Delta\in T,

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=\frac{\partial^{2}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}.

(2)

For
\{\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}\}\subset T,

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta_{1}}\partial\xi_{\Delta_{2}}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=0.

(3)

For
e\in E
and
\Delta\in T,

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{\partial\alpha_{e}\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=-\frac{\partial^{2}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}.

(4)

For
e\in E,

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{\partial\alpha_{e}^{2}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=4\mathbf{i}\frac{\partial\theta_{e}}{\partial l_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}}+\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\frac{\partial^{2}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\bigg(\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}\bigg)^{2}.

(5)

For
\{e_{i},e_{j}\}\subset E,

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=4\mathbf{i}\frac{\partial\theta_{e_{i}}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}}+\sum_{\Delta\sim e_{i},e_{j}}\frac{\partial^{2}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}.

We will prove these claims (1) – (5) at the end. Assuming them, we have

\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}_{s}^{*})=S\cdot D\cdot S^{T},

(4.12)

where
D
is a block matrix with the left-top block the
|E|\times|E|
matrix

\bigg(4\mathbf{i}\frac{\partial\theta_{e_{i}}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}}\bigg)_{e_{i},e_{j}\in E},

the right-top and the left-bottom blocks consisting of
0
’s, and the right-bottom block the
|T|\times|T|
diagonal matrix with the diagonal entries

\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})};
and
S
is a block matrix with the left-top and the right-bottom blocks respectively the
|E|\times|E|
and
|T|\times|T|
identity matrices, the left-bottom block consisting of
0
’s and the right-top block the
|E|\times|T|
matrix with entries
s_{e,\Delta},

e\in E
and
\Delta\in T,
given by

s_{e,\Delta}=-\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}

if
\Delta\sim e,
and
s_{e,\Delta}=0

if otherwise.
Then

\begin{split}\det D=&\det\bigg(4\mathbf{i}\frac{\partial\theta_{e_{i}}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}}\bigg)_{e_{i},e_{j}\in E}\prod_{\Delta\in T}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\\
=&(-1)^{\frac{|E|}{2}}2^{2|E|}\det\bigg(\frac{\partial\theta_{i}}{\partial l_{j}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s}}\bigg)_{e_{i},e_{j}\in E}\prod_{\Delta\in T}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})};\end{split}

(4.13)

and since
S
is upper-triangular with all diagonal entries equal to
1,

\det S=1.

(4.14)

Putting (
4.12
), (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) together, we have

\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}^{*}_{s})\big)=(-1)^{\frac{|E|}{2}}2^{2|E|}\mathrm{det}\bigg(\frac{\partial\theta_{e_{i}}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}_{s}^{*}}\bigg)_{e_{i},e_{j}\in E}\prod_{\Delta\in T}\bigg(-\frac{\partial^{2}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta}}\bigg).

Then by Proposition
3.2
and Theorem
2.21
,

\begin{split}\frac{\det\big(-\mathrm{Hess}\mathcal{W}(\bm{z}_{s}^{*})\big)}{\exp\big(\frac{\kappa(\bm{z}_{s}^{*})}{\pi\mathbf{i}}\big)}=&(-1)^{\frac{|E|}{2}}2^{2|E|+4|T|}\mathrm{det}\bigg(\frac{\partial\theta_{e_{i}}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}_{s}^{*}}\bigg)\prod_{\Delta\in T}\sqrt{\det\mathrm{Gram}(\bm{l}^{*}_{s,\Delta})}\\
=&\pm 2^{3|E|+|T|}\mathbb{T}_{(N,\bm{m})}([\rho_{s}]).\end{split}

This proves (
4.11
) under the assumption that claims (1) – (5) hold.

Now we prove the claims (1) – (5). Claims (1) and (2) follow straightforwardly from the definition of
\mathcal{W}.

For (3), we have

\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}=\frac{\partial U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}.

(4.15)

Let
f(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})\doteq\frac{\partial U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}

and
g(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\doteq f(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})).

Then

g(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=\frac{\partial U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}=\frac{dU_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}}{d\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}\equiv 0,

and hence

\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=0

(4.16)

for all
e\sim\Delta.

On the other hand, for
e\sim\Delta
we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=&\frac{\partial f}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\\
=&\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e}\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}+\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}.\end{split}

(4.17)

Putting (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) together, we have

\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e}\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}=-\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}},

(4.18)

and (3) follows from (
4.15
) and (
4.18
).

For (4) and (5), we have for
e\in E

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}}{\partial\alpha_{e}^{2}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta})},

(4.19)

and for
\{e_{i},e_{j}\}\subset T

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{W}}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{z}^{*}_{s}}=\sum_{\Delta\sim e_{i},e_{j}}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta},\xi^{*}_{s,\Delta)}}.

(4.20)

Let
W
be the function defined in (
3.11
). By the Chain Rule and (
3.10
), we have

\frac{\partial U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}=\frac{dU_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}}{d\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}=0,

and hence for
e\sim\Delta
we have

\frac{\partial W}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=\frac{\partial U}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}+\frac{\partial U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=\frac{\partial U}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}.

Then using the Chain Rule again, for
e_{i},e_{j}\sim\Delta
we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}+\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}.\end{split}

Together with (
4.18
), for
e_{i},e_{j}\sim\Delta
we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}=&\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}-\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\\
=&\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}+\frac{\partial^{2}U}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}^{2}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}))}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\frac{\partial\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}.\end{split}

(4.21)

By Proposition
3.1
, we have

\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\alpha_{e_{i}}\partial\alpha_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{s,\Delta}}=4\mathbf{i}\frac{\partial\theta_{(\Delta,e_{i})}}{\partial l_{e_{j}}}\bigg|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{s,\Delta}},

where
\theta_{(\Delta,e_{i})}
is the dihedral angle of
\Delta
at
e_{i}
as a function of
\bm{l}_{\Delta},

Then (4) and (5) follow from (
4.19
), (
4.20
), (
4.21
) and the fact that

\theta_{e}=\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\theta_{(\Delta,e)},

where the sum is over all the corners around
e.

This completes the proof of the claims (1) – (5).
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

The functions
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}
is strictly concave down in
(\mathrm{Im}\bm{\alpha},\mathrm{Im}\bm{\xi})
on
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}\times D^{T}.

Proof.

Since
2\pi\sum_{e\in E}\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}
is a linear function, it suffice to prove that for each
\Delta\in T,

\mathrm{Im}U
is strictly concave down in
(\mathrm{Im}\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta})
on
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{6}\times D,
so that their sum is a strictly concave down function.

To this end, we will show that:

(a)

-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathrm{Im}L(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})
is linear in
\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{6},
and

(b)

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathrm{Im}L(\xi-\tau_{i})+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathrm{Im}L(\eta_{j}-\xi)
is strictly concave down in
\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{6}
and
\mathrm{Im}\xi.

Then by (
3.3
),
\mathrm{Im}U
is strictly concave down in
\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{6}
and
\mathrm{Im}\xi.

To see (a), for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\},
suppose
\eta_{j}-\tau_{i}=\alpha_{l}+\alpha_{m}-\alpha_{n}
for some
\{l,m,n\}\subset\{1,\dots,6\},
then by a direct computation, for
s,t\in\{l,m,n\},

-\frac{\partial^{2}\mathrm{Im}L(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{s}\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{t}}=\pm\frac{4\sin\big(2\mathrm{Re}(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})\big)}{\big|1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})}\big|^{2}}=0,

where the last equality come from
\mathrm{Re}(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})=\frac{\pi}{2}.
For
i\in\{1,2,3,4\},
suppose
\tau_{i}=\alpha_{l}+\alpha_{m}+\alpha_{n}
for some
\{l,m,n\}\subset\{1,\dots,6\},
then by a direct computation, for
s,t\in\{l,m,n\},

-\frac{\partial^{2}\mathrm{Im}L(2\pi-\tau_{i})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{s}\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{t}}=-\frac{4\sin\big(2\mathrm{Re}(2\pi-\tau_{i})\big)}{\big|1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(2\pi-\tau_{i})}\big|^{2}}=0,

where the last equality comes from
\mathrm{Re}(2\pi-\tau_{i})=\frac{\pi}{2}.

As a consequence,
-\mathrm{Im}L(\eta_{j}-\tau_{i})
’s are linear in
\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{6},
from which (a) follows.

To see (b), we make the following change of variables: For
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
let
x_{i}=\xi-\tau_{i},
and for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
let
x_{j+4}=\eta_{j}-\xi.
Then
we have
2\pi-\xi=2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k},
and

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathrm{Im}L(\xi-\tau_{i})+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathrm{Im}L(\eta_{j}-\xi)=\sum_{k=1}^{7}\mathrm{Im}L(x_{k})+L\Big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\Big).

We will show that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,7\},

\mathrm{Im}L(x_{k})
is strictly concave down in
\mathrm{Im}x_{k};
and
\mathrm{Im}L\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)
is concave down in
\mathrm{Im}x_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}x_{7}.
Then from this, (2) follows.

Indeed, for each
k\in\{1,\dots,7\},
by a direct computation we have

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathrm{Im}L(x_{k})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}x_{k}^{2}}=-\frac{4\sin\big(2\mathrm{Re}x_{k}\big)}{\big|1-e^{2\mathbf{i}x_{k}}\big|^{2}}<0,

where the inequality comes from that
\mathrm{Re}x_{k}
equals either
\mathrm{Re}(\xi-\tau_{i})
or
\mathrm{Re}(\eta_{j}-\xi),
in either case is in
(0,\pi).
As a consequence,
\mathrm{Im}L(x_{k})
is strictly concave down in
\mathrm{Im}x_{k}.
For all
k,l\in\{1,\dots,7\},
by a direct computation we have

\frac{\partial^{2}\mathrm{Im}L\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}x_{k}\partial\mathrm{Im}x_{l}}=-\frac{4\sin\big(2\mathrm{Re}\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)\big)}{\big|1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k})}\big|^{2}}<0,

where the inequality comes from that
\mathrm{Re}\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)=\mathrm{Re}(2\pi-\xi)\in(0,\pi).
Therefore, the Hessian matrix of
\mathrm{Im}L\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)
with respect to
\mathrm{Im}x_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}x_{7}
is a
7\times 7
matrix with all of its entries the same negative constant, hence is negative semi-definite. As a consequence,
\mathrm{Im}L\big(2\pi-\sum_{k=1}^{7}x_{k}\big)
is concave down in
\mathrm{Im}x_{1},\dots,\mathrm{Im}x_{7}.

∎

For any ideal triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of
M
with the space of angle structures
\mathcal{A}(M,\mathcal{T})\neq\emptyset,
by Corollary
2.13
with the constant
t_{0}>0
therein, there is a generalized hyperbolic polyhedral metric
\bm{l}_{t_{0}}^{*}=\big(l^{*}_{t_{0},e}\big)
whose cone angles are
2\pi+t_{0}
at all the edges.

For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let

\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{1}}}{2},\dots,\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{6}}}{2}\Big).

Guaranteed by Proposition
3.1
, we have

(1)

if
(l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{6}})\in\mathcal{L},
then we let
\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}
be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}
in
D;

(2)

if
(l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{6}})\in\partial\mathcal{L},
then we let
\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}
be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}
on
\partial D;
and

(3)

if
(l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{6}})\in{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}^{6}{\smallsetminus}(\mathcal{L}\cup\partial\mathcal{L}),
then we can choose from the two a critical point
\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}

of
U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}
on
\partial D.
For consistency, we choose the one corresponding to
\xi_{1}^{*}
in Proposition
3.1
(3).

By Proposition
3.1
(2) and (3),
\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}=2\pi
in the last two cases.

Let
\overline{D}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ \frac{3\pi}{2}\leqslant\mathrm{Re}\xi\leqslant 2\pi\}.
Then we have the following

Proposition 4.5
.

Let

\mathcal{U}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=(2\pi+{t_{0}})\sum_{e\in E}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big).

Then
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}
has a critical point

\bm{z}^{*}_{t_{0}}=\bigg(\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\frac{l^{*}_{t_{0},e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E},\big(\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}\bigg)

in
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}\times\overline{D}^{T}.

Proof.

Let
\bm{z}^{*}_{t_{0}}=(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}}),
where
\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}}=\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{l^{*}_{t_{0},e}}{2}\Big)_{e\in E}
and
\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}}=\big(\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}\big)_{\Delta\in T}.
For
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6},
let
l^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}=(l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{1}},\dots,l^{*}_{t_{0},e_{6}}).

We first have

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}})}=\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0},\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}=0.

(4.22)

Now let
W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta})=U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi^{*}_{\Delta})
be the function defined in (
3.11
). Then for any edge
e
of
\Delta,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}=&\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}+\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}\\
=&\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}.\end{split}

As a consequence, and by Proposition
3.1
(1) (2) and (3)

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}=\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}W(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}=-4\frac{\partial\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{l}_{\Delta})}{\partial l_{e}}\Big|_{\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}=-2\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)},

(4.23)

where

\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)}
is the extended dihedral angle of the
\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}
at the edge
e.
Then for each
e\in E,

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}})}=2(2\pi+{t_{0}})+\sum_{\Delta\in T}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}=2\Big((2\pi+{t_{0}})-\sum_{((\Delta,e))\in C}\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)}\Big)=0,

(4.24)

where the last summation is over all the corners around
e,
and the last equality comes from the fact that the sum of the dihedral angles around an edge equals is the cone angle at this edge, which equals
2\pi(1+{t_{0}}).
By (
4.22
) and (
4.24
),
(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}})
is a critical point of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}.

∎

In the generalized hyperbolic cone metric
\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0}},
we call a tetrahedron
\Delta

degenerate
if
\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}\in{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}{\smallsetminus}\mathcal{L}.
For
c\in(\frac{3\pi}{2},2\pi),
let
\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta}
the unique maximum point of
\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}
on
\Gamma_{c}
guaranteed by Corollary
3.5
. For a corner
(\Delta,e),
let
\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)}=\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)}
be the original dihedral angle of
\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0}}
if
\Delta
is non-degenerate, and let

\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta})}

if
\Delta
is degenerate. For
e\in E,
let

\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}=\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)},

(4.25)

where the sum is over all the corners around
e.
Let
\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}\in D^{T}
be obtained from
\bm{\xi}^{*}_{t_{0}}\in\overline{D}^{T}
by replacing the component
\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}
by
\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta}
for each degenerate
\Delta
in
T.
We note that, as
c
tends to
2\pi,

\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)}
converges to
\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)}
for each corner
(\Delta,e),

and therefore we have

\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}\to\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},e}=2\pi+t_{0}

(4.26)

for each edge
e.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let

\mathcal{U}_{c}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{e\in E}\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big).

Then
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}
has a unique maximum point
\bm{z}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}=(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}})
on the contour

\Gamma^{*}_{c,t_{0}}=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times D^{T}\ \bigg|\ \mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}=\mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}\bigg\}.

Proof.

First, by Corollary
3.5
, if
\Delta
is non-degenerate, we have

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}})}=\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0},\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}}=0;

and if
\Delta
is degenerate, by the choice of
\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta},
we have

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}})}=\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0},\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big|_{\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta}}=0.

Next, if
\Delta
is non-degenerate, then by (
4.23
), we have

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}=-2\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},(\Delta,e)}=-2\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)};

and if
\Delta
is degenerate, then by definition

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta})}=-2\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)}.

Now for each
e\in E,

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{U}_{c}}{\partial\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}})}\\
=&2\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}+\sum_{\Delta\text{ non-degenerate}}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta})}+\sum_{\Delta\text{ degenerate}}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\Big|_{(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta},\xi^{*}_{c,t_{0},\Delta})}\\
=&2\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}-2\sum_{\Delta\sim e}\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},(\Delta,e)}=0.\end{split}

Therefore,
\bm{z}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}
is a critical point of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}
on
\Gamma^{*}_{c}.

Finally, since
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}
and
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}
differ by a linear function
\sum_{e\in E}(\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}-2\pi)l_{e},
they share the same concavity; and by Proposition
4.4
,
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}
is strictly concave down in
(\mathrm{Im}\bm{\alpha},\mathrm{Im}\bm{\xi})
on
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}\mathbb{R}\big)^{E}\times D^{T}.
As a consequence,
\bm{z}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}
is the unique critical point of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c}
on
\Gamma^{*}_{c,t_{0}},
and achieves the maximum value.
∎

For
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{6},

\delta>0
sufficiently small and
c\in(\delta,\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta),
recall that
D^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\delta,c}
is the region depicted in Figure
5
and defined there-around consisting of
\xi\in\mathbb{C}
either with
\mathrm{Re}\xi\in[\frac{3\pi}{2}+\delta,2\pi-\delta]
or with
|\mathrm{Im}(\xi-\tau_{i})|\geqslant\delta,

|\mathrm{Im}(\eta_{j}-\xi)|\geqslant\delta
for all
i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
\mathrm{Re}\xi\in[\frac{3\pi}{2}-c,2\pi+c].
We consider the following region

\mathcal{D}_{\delta,c}=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times\mathbb{C}^{T}\ \bigg|\ \xi_{\Delta}\in D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c}\text{ for all }\Delta\in T\bigg\}.

We notice that if
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\mathcal{D}_{\delta,c},
then for all
\Delta\in T,
all
\xi_{\Delta}-\tau_{i}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
and
\eta_{j}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})-\xi_{\Delta},

i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\},
are in the region
H_{\delta,K}=\big\{x\in\mathbb{C}\ |\ \delta\leqslant\mathrm{Re}x\leqslant\pi-\delta,\text{ or }|\mathrm{Re}x|\leqslant K\text{ and }|\mathrm{Im}x|\geqslant\delta\big\}
depicted in Figure
2
for any
K>c.

Proposition 4.7
.

There exists a constant
K>0
such that, for all unit vector
\mathbf{u}
of the form

\mathbf{u}=\bigg(\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{e}}\bigg)_{e\in E},\bigg(\Big(0,\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}\Big)\bigg)_{\Delta\in T}\bigg)

in
\mathbb{R}^{|E|+2|T|}
identified with the tangent space of
\mathcal{D}_{\delta,c}
at each of its points,
the directional derivative

D_{\mathbf{u}}\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})<K

for all for
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})
in
\mathcal{D}_{\delta,c}.

Proof.

As argued in the Proof of Proposition
3.10
, for
x\in H_{\delta,K},
there exists a constant
C_{\delta,K}>0
depending on
\delta
and
K
such that

\bigg|\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}x}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}x}\bigg|<C_{\delta,K}.

Then by a direction computation, we have for
k\in\{1,\dots,6\},

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{k}}=&14\pi-4\pi\sum_{\tau_{i}\sim\alpha_{k}}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\xi-\tau_{i})}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{k}}\bigg)+3\pi\sum_{\eta_{j}\sim\alpha_{k}}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\eta_{j}-\xi}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\\
\leqslant&14\pi+4\pi\sum_{\tau_{i}\sim\alpha_{k}}\bigg|\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\xi-\tau_{i})}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{k}}\bigg|+3\pi\sum_{\eta_{j}\sim\alpha_{k}}\bigg|\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\eta_{j}-\xi}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{k}}\bigg|<K_{1}\end{split}

for all
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{6}
and
\xi\in D^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\delta,c},

where the sums are respectively over the two
\tau_{i}
’s and the two
\eta_{j}
’s containing
\alpha_{k},
and

K_{1}=14\pi+4\pi\sum_{\tau_{i}\sim\alpha_{k}}C_{\delta,c}+3\pi\sum_{\eta_{j}\sim\alpha_{k}}C_{\delta,c}=14\pi+14\pi C_{\delta,c}.

Also by a direct computation,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}=&-28\pi-4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\xi-\tau_{i})}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\bigg)+3\pi\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\eta_{j}-\xi}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\bigg)\\
\leqslant&-28\pi+4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{4}\bigg|\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\xi-\tau_{i})}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\bigg|+3\pi\sum_{j=1}^{4}\bigg|\frac{\partial\log\big(1-e^{2\mathbf{i}(\eta_{j}-\xi}\big)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\bigg|<K_{2}\end{split}

for all
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{6}
and
\xi\in D^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\delta,c},
where

K_{2}=-28\pi+4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{4}C_{\delta,c}+3\pi\sum_{j=1}^{4}C_{\delta,c}=-28\pi+28\pi C_{\delta,c}.

As a consequence, for any vector
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{7}
with norm
|\mathbf{u}|\leqslant 1,
the directional derivative

D_{\mathbf{u}}\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)=\Big\langle\mathbf{u},\Big(\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\alpha_{6}},\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\xi)}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi}\Big)\Big\rangle<\max\{K_{1},K_{2}\}

(4.27)

for all
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{6}
and
\xi\in D^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\delta,c}.

Now for a unit vector
\mathbf{u}=\big((u_{e})_{e\in E},(u_{\Delta})_{\Delta\in T}\big)\in\mathbb{R}^{|E|+2|T|}
of the form in the statement of the proposition, let
\mathbf{u}_{\Delta}=(u_{e_{1}},\dots,u_{e_{6}},u_{\Delta})
for a
\Delta\in T
with edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{6}.

Then by (
4.27
), we have

D_{\mathbf{u}}\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{\Delta\in T}D_{\mathbf{u}_{\Delta}}\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})<K

for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\mathcal{D}_{\delta,c},
where
K=|T|\max\{K_{1},K_{2}\}.

∎

More generally, for
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{C}^{6},

\delta>0
sufficiently small and
c\in(\delta,\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta),
recall that
D^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\delta,c}
is the region defined before Proposition
3.11
consisting of
\xi\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathrm{Re}(\xi-\tau_{i})\geqslant\delta,

|\mathrm{Im}(\xi-\tau_{i})|\geqslant\delta
for all
i\in\{1,2,3,4\},

\mathrm{Re}(\eta_{j}-\xi)\geqslant\delta,

|\mathrm{Im}(\eta_{j}-\xi)|\geqslant\delta
for all
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
\mathrm{Re}\xi\in[\frac{3\pi}{2}-c,2\pi+c].
We consider the region

\mathbf{D}_{\delta,c}=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\Big[\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta,\frac{\pi}{2}+\delta\Big]+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times\mathbb{C}^{T}\ \bigg|\ \xi_{\Delta}\in D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c}\text{ for all }\Delta\in T\bigg\}.

We notice that if
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbf{D}_{\delta,c},
then for all
\Delta\in T,
all
\xi_{\Delta}-\tau_{i}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
and
\eta_{j}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})-\xi_{\Delta},

i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\},
are in the region
H_{\delta,K}
depicted in Figure
2
and defined there around for any
K>c.

Proposition 4.8
.

For
b>0
and
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
c\in(\delta,\frac{\pi}{2}-\delta),
there exists a constant
N>0
independent of
b
such that

\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{\nu}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\leqslant\big|\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\big|<N

for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbf{D}_{\delta,c}.

Proof.

Let
N_{\delta,c}>0
be the constant as in Proposition
3.11
for the chosen
\delta
and
c.

Then by (
3.24
) and (
4.2
),

\begin{split}\big|\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\big|=&\Big|\frac{\mathcal{W}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}-\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{b^{4}}\Big|\\
\leqslant&\sum_{\Delta\in T}\Big|\frac{U_{b}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})-\kappa_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})b^{2}-U(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{b^{4}}\Big|<N\end{split}

for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbf{D}_{\delta,c},
where
N=|T|N_{\delta,c}.

∎

4.2
State-integral convergence with angled triangulations and proof of Theorem
1.6

Theorem
1.6
is an immediate consequence of the following Theorem
4.9
and Proposition
2.16
.
Theorem
4.9
will also be needed in the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
M
be a hyperbolic
3
-manifold with totally geodesic boundary and let
\mathcal{T}
be an ideal triangulation of
M
that supports an angle structure (see section
2.7
). Then there exists a
b_{0}\in(0,1)
such that for all
b<b_{0},
the integral

\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})=\int_{\big(\frac{Q}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}}\prod_{e\in E}|e|_{\bm{a}}\prod_{\Delta\in T}|\Delta|_{\bm{a}}d\mathrm{Im}\bm{a}

converges absolutely.

Proof.

By Corollary
2.13
and with the constant
t_{0}>0
therein, there exists
\bm{l}^{*}_{t_{0}}
a generalized hyperbolic polyhedral metric on
(M,\mathcal{T})
with cone angles
2\pi+t_{0}
at all the edges. Let

\mathcal{U}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=(2\pi+t_{0})\sum_{e\in E}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)

be the function in Proposition
4.5
whose imaginary part has a critical point
\bm{z}_{t_{0}}^{*}=\big(\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*},\bm{\xi}_{t_{0}}^{*}\big)

in
\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}\times\overline{D}^{T}.

For such
t_{0}
and
c\in(\frac{3\pi}{2},2\pi),
we let

\mathcal{U}_{c}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\sum_{e\in E}\widetilde{\theta}_{c,t_{0},e}(2\alpha_{e}-\pi)+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta}\big)

be the function defined in Proposition
4.6
whose imaginary part has the unique maximum point
\bm{z}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}=(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}})
on the contour

\Gamma^{*}_{c,t_{0}}=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times D^{T}\ \bigg|\ \mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}=\mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c,t_{0}}\bigg\}.

Since
\widetilde{\theta}_{c_{0},t_{0},e}
converges to
\widetilde{\theta}_{t_{0},e}=2\pi+t_{0}
as
c
tends to
2\pi,
we can choose a
c_{0}\in(\frac{3\pi}{2},2\pi)
sufficiently close to
2\pi
such that

\widetilde{\theta}_{c_{0},t_{0},e}>2\pi+\frac{t_{0}}{2}

(4.28)

for each
e\in E.

For each
\Delta\in T,
we consider the contour

\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0},\Delta}=\Big\{\xi_{\Delta}\in D\ \Big|\ \mathrm{Re}\xi_{\Delta}=\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0},\Delta}\Big\}

and
consider

\begin{split}\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}=&\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times D^{T}\ \bigg|\ \mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}=\mathrm{Re}\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}\bigg\}\\
=&\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\times\prod_{\Delta\in T}\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0},\Delta}.\end{split}

By (
4.3
), it suffices to show that for each
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\lambda}
in
\{-1,1\}^{E},
there is a
b_{0}>0
such that for any
b<b_{0}
the integral

\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\mu_{e}\lambda_{e}\bigg)\exp\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}

converges.

For the case that
\bm{\mu}=\bm{\lambda}=(1,\dots,1),
we have

\mathcal{V}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\sum_{e\in E}\big(2\pi(1+b^{2})-\widetilde{\theta}_{c_{0},t_{0},e}\big)(2\alpha_{e}-\pi);

and as a consequence of (
4.28
),
we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=&\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\sum_{e\in E}\big(2\pi(1+b^{2})-\widetilde{\theta}_{c_{0},t_{0},e}\big)l_{e}\\
<&\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\Big(2\pi b^{2}-\frac{t_{0}}{2}\Big)\sum_{e\in E}l_{e}\\
<&\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\end{split}

(4.29)

for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}
and
b<\sqrt{\frac{t_{0}}{4\pi}}.

Next, for
L>0,
let

B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L)=\Big\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}\ \Big|\ \big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})\big|\leqslant L\Big\}

and let

\partial B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L)=\big\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}\ \big|\ |(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})|=L\big\}.

Then as a consequence of Proposition
4.4
, the directional derivative of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
is strictly decreasing along each ray in
\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}\cong{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}^{E}\times\mathbb{R}^{T}
starting from
\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}};
and by the compactness of the spheres in
\mathbb{R}^{|E|+|T|},
there is an
L_{0}>0
such that

\max\big\{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\ \big|\ \partial B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})\big\}<-2\mathrm{Vol}(M)-4\epsilon_{0}

(4.30)

for a given
\epsilon_{0}>0,
and for any unit vector
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{|E|+|T|}

the directional derivative

D_{\mathbf{u}}\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}+l\mathbf{u})<-2\epsilon

(4.31)

for all
l>L_{0}
and for some sufficiently small
\epsilon>0.

As
B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})
is bounded, we have

\Bigg|\int_{B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|<\infty.

(4.32)

On the other hand, by (
4.4
), we have

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|\\
\leqslant&\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|.\end{split}

By the compactness of
\partial B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0}),
there is a
b_{0}<\sqrt{\frac{t_{0}}{4\pi}}
such that for all
b<b_{0},

\max\big\{\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\cdot b^{2}\ \big|\ (\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\partial B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})\big\}<\epsilon_{0},

(4.33)

Kb^{2}<\epsilon
and
Nb^{4}<\epsilon_{0},
where
K
and
N
are respectively the constants in Proposition
4.7
and Proposition
4.8
. We claim that, on
\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})
and for
b<b_{0},

\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}<-2\Big(\mathrm{Vol}(M)+\epsilon_{0}\Big)-\epsilon\Big(\big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})\big|-L_{0}\Big),

and as a consequence we have

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}+\mathrm{Im}\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|\\
<&\exp\bigg(\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)-\epsilon_{0}}{\pi b^{2}}\bigg)\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})}\exp\Bigg(\frac{-\epsilon\big(|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})|-L_{0}\big)}{2\pi b^{2}}\Bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|\\
=&\ O\bigg(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)-\epsilon_{0}}{\pi b^{2}}}\bigg).\end{split}

(4.34)

In particular, the integral is finite.

To prove the claim, for
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0}),
we let
(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}^{\prime})
be the intersection of

\partial B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0})

with the line segment connecting
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})
and
(\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}).
Then

\big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})\big|-L_{0}=\big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}^{\prime})\big|\to+\infty

(4.35)

as
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\to\infty.
Let
\mathbf{u}=\frac{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})}{|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})|}.
Then by (
4.31
) and the choice of
b_{0},
for
l>L_{0},
i.e.,
\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}+l\mathbf{u}
in
\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0}),
we have

D_{\mathbf{u}}\Big(\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0},t_{0}}(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}+l\mathbf{u})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}+l\mathbf{u})b^{2}\Big)<-2\epsilon+\epsilon=-\epsilon

for all
b<b_{0}.
Together with the Mean Value Theorem, (
4.30
) and (
4.33
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{2}<&\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}^{\prime})+\mathrm{Im}\kappa(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}^{\prime})b^{2}-\epsilon\big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\xi}^{\prime})\big|\\
<&-2\mathrm{Vol}(M)-3\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon\Big(\big|(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},\bm{\xi}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}})\big|-L_{0}\Big)\end{split}

(4.36)

for all for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0}).
Finally, by Proposition
4.8
and the choice of
b_{0}
, we have

\mathrm{Im}\nu_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})b^{4}<Nb^{4}<\epsilon_{0}

(4.37)

for all
b<b_{0}
and for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}.

Putting (
4.29
), (
4.36
) and (
4.37
) together, we have the inequality in (
4.34
); and by (
4.35
) we have the equality in (
4.34
).

Finally, putting (
4.32
) and (
4.34
) together ,we have

\Bigg|\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|<\infty.

(4.38)

For the case
\bm{\mu}\neq(1,\dots,1)
or
\bm{\lambda}\neq(1,\dots,1),
we observe that

\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=-2\pi\sum_{e\in E}\big((1-\mu_{e})b^{2}+(1-\lambda_{e})\big)l_{e}\leqslant 0

for all for
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})
in
\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}.
Then

\begin{split}\Bigg|\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\mu_{e}\lambda_{e}\bigg)\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|\leqslant&\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|\\
\leqslant&\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|,\end{split}

which is finite by (
4.38
). This, together with (
4.3
), completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Combining Proposition
2.16
and Theorem
4.9
gives Theorem
1.6
.
∎

4.3
Proof of Theorem
1.9

In this subsection we will show that for any Kojima ideal triangulation
\mathcal{T}
,
\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})
has the desired asymptotics in (
1.8
); and in the next section we will show that
\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})
does not depend on
\mathcal{T}
with a separate argument. We present this proof before that of Theorem
1.7
because the style of the argument here is close to the ones in the previous section.

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a Kojima ideal triangulation of
M
with the set of edges
E
and the set of tetrahedra
T.
Then by (
1.7
) and (
3.6
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})=\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}4\sinh l_{e}\sinh\frac{l_{e}}{b^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg(\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\xi}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha},\end{split}

(4.39)

where for each
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{Q}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E},

\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}}=\prod_{\Delta\in T}\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}},
and each
\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
is a contour of integral for
\xi_{\Delta}
that is to be specified.
Let
L_{0}
be as in the proof of Theorem
4.9
,
and let

B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})=\Big\{\bm{\alpha}\in\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}\ \Big|\ |\bm{\alpha}-\bm{\alpha}_{t_{0}}^{*}|\leqslant L_{0}\Big\}.

Then

\mathrm{TV}_{b}(M,\mathcal{T})=\mathrm{I}_{\text{out}}+\mathrm{I}_{\text{in}},

where

\mathrm{I}_{\text{out}}=\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}4\sinh l_{e}\sinh\frac{l_{e}}{b^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg(\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\xi}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha},

(4.40)

and

\mathrm{I}_{\text{in}}=\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}4\sinh l_{e}\sinh\frac{l_{e}}{b^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg(\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\xi}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}.

(4.41)

We will show that

(I)

\mathrm{I}_{\text{out}}<O\bigg(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)-\epsilon_{1}}{\pi b^{2}}}\bigg)

for some
\epsilon_{1}>0,
and

(II)

\mathrm{I}_{\text{in}}=(2\mathbf{i})^{\frac{\chi(M)}{2}}\frac{e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)}{\pi b^{2}}}}{\sqrt{\pm\mathrm{Tor}(DM,\mathrm{Ad}_{\rho_{DM}})}}\Big(1+O\big(b^{2}\big)\Big).

Then putting (I) and (II) together, we have the result.

To prove (I), for all
\bm{\alpha}\in\big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\big)^{E}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
and
\Delta\in T,
we identically choose
\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
to be

\Gamma^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}=\Big\{\xi_{\Delta}\in D\ \Big|\ \mathrm{Re}\xi_{\Delta}=\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}\Big\}

as in the proof of Theorem
1.6
, and let

\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}=\bigg(\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}+\mathbf{i}{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\Big)^{E}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})\bigg)\times\prod_{\Delta\in T}\Gamma^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}.

Then by (
4.40
),

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{\text{out}}=&\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}4\sinh l_{e}\sinh\frac{l_{e}}{b^{2}}\bigg)\exp\Bigg(\frac{\sum_{\Delta\in T}U_{b}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi_{\Delta})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\\
=&\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\sum_{\bm{\mu}\in\{-1,1\}^{E}}\sum_{\bm{\lambda}\in\{-1,1\}^{E}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\mu_{e}\lambda_{e}\bigg)\exp\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\Bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}.\end{split}

(4.42)

For
\bm{\mu}=\bm{\lambda}=(1,\dots,1),
since
\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}\subset\Gamma^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}}{\smallsetminus}B(\bm{z}^{*}_{c_{0},t_{0}},L_{0}),
as a consequence of (
4.34
), we have

\Bigg|\frac{(-2\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|<\ O\bigg(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)-\epsilon_{1}}{\pi b^{2}}}\bigg)

(4.43)

for any
\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{0},
where
\epsilon_{0}
is as in the proof of (1).
For
\bm{\mu}\neq(1,\dots,1)
or
\bm{\lambda}\neq(1,\dots,1),
we have

\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})-\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})=-2\pi\sum_{e\in E}\big((1-\mu_{e})b^{2}+(1-\lambda_{e})\big)l_{e}\leqslant 0

for all for
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})
in
\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}.
Then by (
4.43
), for any
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\lambda}
in
\{-1,1\}^{E},

we have

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\frac{(-\mathbf{i})^{|E|}}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\bigg(\prod_{e\in E}\mu_{e}\lambda_{e}\bigg)\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{b}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi\mathbf{i}b^{2}}\bigg)d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}\Bigg|\\
\leqslant&\frac{1}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}^{\bm{\mu}\bm{\lambda}}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|\\
\leqslant&\frac{1}{(\pi b)^{|E|+|T|}}\int_{\Gamma^{*}_{\text{out}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{V}_{b}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})}{2\pi b^{2}}\bigg)|d\bm{\alpha}d\bm{\xi}|\\
<&\ O\bigg(e^{\frac{-\mathrm{Vol}(M)-\epsilon_{1}}{\pi b^{2}}}\bigg);\end{split}

(4.44)

and by (
4.42
), we have (I).

To prove (II), for each
\bm{\alpha}\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
and
\Delta\in T,
we will choose the contour
\Gamma^{*}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}

as follows. Let
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=2\mathrm{Im}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}).
If
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\in\mathcal{L}\cup\partial\mathcal{L},
then we let

\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=\xi^{*}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})

be the unique critical point of
U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
in
\overline{D}
guaranteed by Proposition
3.1
(1) (2); and if
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\in{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}^{6}{\smallsetminus}(\mathcal{L}\cup\partial\mathcal{L}),

then we let

\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=\xi^{*}_{1}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\cup\xi^{*}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})

be the union of the two critical points

\xi^{*}_{1}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
and
\xi^{*}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
of
U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
on
\partial\Delta
guaranteed by Proposition
3.1
(3).
In the latter case, by Proposition
3.1
(3) again, we have

\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{1}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})=2\pi

and

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\big(\xi^{*}_{1}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\big)=\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\big(\xi^{*}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\big)=-2\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}).

As such, we define

\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\doteq 2\pi

and

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\big(\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\big)\doteq-2\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}})

for each
\Delta\in T
with
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\in{\mathbb{R}_{>0}}^{6}{\smallsetminus}(\mathcal{L}\cup\partial\mathcal{L}).

Then we let

\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=\Big\{\xi_{\Delta}\in\overline{D}\ \Big|\ \mathrm{Re}\xi_{\Delta}=\mathrm{Re}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\Big\},

and let

\Gamma=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})\times\overline{D}^{T}\ \bigg|\ \xi_{\Delta}\in\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\text{ for all }\Delta\text{ in }T\bigg\}.

We notice that, for a
\Delta\in T,
if
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\notin\mathcal{L},
then
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
passes through
\eta_{j}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
’s which are the singular points of
U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}.
As a consequence, if there are flat tetrahedra in
T,
then
\Gamma
will pass through the singular points of
\mathcal{W}.
Hence we need to deform
\Gamma
to a new contour
\Gamma^{\prime}
to avoid these singular points.
By the compactness of
B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0}),
there is a
\delta>0
such that Corollary
3.7
holds for any
\bm{\alpha}\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
and for any
\xi_{\Delta}
in the
\delta
-square neighborhood of
\eta_{j}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})
’s. Now for
\bm{\alpha}\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
and
\Delta\in T,
we consider a deformation
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
of
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
defined as follows. If
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
does not intersect the complement of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c},
then we let
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}=\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
(see (1) of Figure
9
); and if
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
intersects the complement of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c},
then we let
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
be the contour obtained from
\Gamma_{\Delta}
by pushing the parts out of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c}
into the boundary of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c}
(see (2), (3) of Figure
9
).

Figure 9
:
Contour
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
when: (1)
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\in\mathcal{L}
and
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
does not intersect the complement of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c},
(2)
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\in\mathcal{L}
and
\Gamma_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
intersects the complement of
D^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}_{\delta,c},
and (3)
\bm{l}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\notin\mathcal{L}.

Let

\Gamma^{\prime}=\bigg\{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})\times\overline{D}^{T}\ \bigg|\ \xi_{\Delta}\in\Gamma^{\prime}_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\text{ for all }\Delta\text{ in }T\bigg\}.

Then
\Gamma^{\prime}
passes through the critical point
(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})
of
\mathcal{W},
and by Proposition
3.1
(1) (2) (3), Proposition
2.15
and Proposition
4.1
, we have

\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\leqslant\sum_{e\in E}2\pi l_{e}+\sum_{\Delta\in T}U\big(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta},\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\big)=-2\widetilde{\mathrm{Cov}}(\bm{l})\leqslant-2\mathrm{Vol}(M)=\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})

(4.45)

for all
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})\in\Gamma^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}\{(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})\},
with the equality held when
\bm{\alpha}=\bm{\alpha}^{*},

\xi_{\Delta}=\xi^{*}_{\Delta}
for all non-flat
\Delta
in
T,
and
\xi_{\Delta}
lies on the line segment connecting
\eta^{*}_{j_{2},\Delta}
and
\eta^{*}_{j_{3},\Delta}
for all flat
\Delta
in
T.
From this we see that, when there are flat tetrahedra in
T,
the critical point
(\bm{\alpha}^{*},\bm{\xi}^{*})
is not the unique maximum point of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}
on
\Gamma^{\prime}.
Therefore, to be able to apply Proposition
2.22
, the Saddle Point Approximation, we need to further deform
\Gamma^{\prime}
to decrease the value of
\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}.
To do this, by Proposition
3.1
(1) (2), Proposition
3.3
(1), Proposition
3.4
and the compactness of
B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
we can choose an
L_{1}>L_{0}
large enough so that: For a given
\epsilon>0,
and for any
\bm{\alpha}\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0}),

\Delta\in T
and
\xi_{\Delta}\in\Gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha_{\Delta}}
with
|\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}-\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}|>L_{1},

(a)

\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})<\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}\big(\xi^{*}_{\Delta}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})\big)-4\epsilon;

(4.46)

(b)

if
\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}>\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}+L_{1},
then

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}<-2\pi,

(4.47)

and if
\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}<\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{\Delta}-L_{1},
then

\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}(\xi_{\Delta})}{\partial\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}}>2\pi;

(4.48)

(c)

and for
\delta>0
as above,

[\mathrm{Im}\eta_{j_{1}}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})-\delta,\mathrm{Im}\eta_{j_{4}}(\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta})+\delta]\subset\big(\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}-L_{1}+\delta,\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}+L_{1}-\delta\big).

(4.49)

Now for each
\bm{\alpha}\in B(\bm{\alpha}^{*}_{t_{0}},L_{0})
and
\Delta\in T,
we consider the following smooth vector filed
\psi_{\Delta}\mathbf{v}_{\Delta}
on

D_{\delta,c}^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}},

where

\psi_{\Delta}
is a

C^{\infty}
-smooth bump function on
D_{\delta,c}^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\psi_{\Delta}(\xi_{\Delta})=1&\text{if}&|\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}-\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}|\leqslant L_{1}-\delta\\
0<\psi_{\Delta}(\xi_{\Delta})<1&\text{if}&L_{1}-\delta<|\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}-\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}|<L_{1}\\
\psi_{\Delta}(\xi_{\Delta})=0&\text{if}&|\mathrm{Im}\xi_{\Delta}-\mathrm{Im}\xi^{*}_{t_{0},\Delta}|\geqslant L_{1}\end{array}\right.

with
\delta>0
as above, and
\mathbf{v}_{\Delta}
is the vector field on
D_{\delta,c}^{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}
defined by

\mathbf{v}_{\Delta}=\bigg(-\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}U_{\bm{\alpha}_{\Delta}}}{\partial\mathrm{Re}\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{\xi_{\Delta}},0\bigg)=\bigg(-\frac{\partial\mathrm{Im}\mathcal{W}}{\partial\mathrm{Re}\xi_{\Delta}}\bigg|_{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\xi})},0\bigg).
